I handle it okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, my mood has been fine. I don't really get down in the dumps or anything like that. So, no, feeling sad isn't really an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any emotional difficulties, such as anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't really feel anxious or nervous about things. I just kind of go about my day. So, no, anxiety hasn't been a problem.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start noticing these mistakes and difficulties with focus?\n\nPatient: Well, it's really been a problem since I moved into the supervisor role at work. But other than that... no, nothing else comes to mind. Everything at home and in the rest of my life has stayed pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: Have these difficulties with focus and making mistakes been consistent over the past year or have they varied in severity?\n\nPatient: They've been pretty consistent, I think. I mean, it's not like some days are perfect and others are terrible. It's just a constant struggle to stay on top of things at work since I took the job. At home, it just... it adds to the conflict, you know? My family, my friends, they feel like they can't count on me to follow through on things I say I'm going to do. And honestly, it's not just outside of work either. It's gotten to the point where my manager actually put it in my formal review. So it's pretty much affecting everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to change your daily routines or activities outside of work to cope with your difficulties with focus and mistakes?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I haven't consciously changed anything about my routine. I still try to do the same things I've always done—go to the gym, hang out with friends, take care of stuff around the house. It's just... harder to get it all done now. But I haven't started doing anything specific to cope with it, if that's what you mean. I'm mostly just trying to keep my head above water at work, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant emotional distress or impairment in social or occupational functioning as a result of your difficulties with focus and mistakes?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure what counts as significant emotional distress. I mean... I'm definitely stressed out, and it's really frustrating. And like I mentioned, it's already causing arguments at home and it showed up on my performance review at work, so it's definitely getting in the way of my life.
